<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to advance the data-to-data generative process in bridge models by introducing a guidance method that can effectively exploit a clean prior, inspired by previous methods that create a quality difference between denoising results as guidance.

Method: The authors propose Prior Guidance (PG) and its variants, including frequency-modulated prior guidance (FMPG) and a cascaded framework CFG-FMPG, which leverage a weak prior and a scaling factor to enhance prior exploitation, and tailor the guidance scale to low- and high-frequency bands coherent with bridge generative dynamics.

Result: Experiments demonstrate that the proposed PG methods consistently improve pre-trained bridge models across diverse image translation tasks.

Conclusion: The authors conclude that their proposed methods can effectively enhance prior exploitation in bridge models, leading to improved performance in image translation tasks.

Abstract: Guidance methods, such as classifier-free guidance (CFG) and auto-guidance (AG), have advanced noise-to-data generation in diffusion models. Recently, bridge models have introduced a data-to-data generative process that can exploit an instructive clean prior. In this work, inspired by previous methods creating quality difference between denoising results as guidance, we propose a training-free bridge guidance method, termed Prior Guidance (PG). Specifically, we introduce a weak prior, which is unseen during bridge pre-training, hindering prior exploitation and thereby degrading denoising result. Then, we contrast it with the seen prior to highlight and enhance prior exploitation via a scaling factor. Moreover, we analyze the underlying mechanism of prior exploitation in the bridge process and design frequency-modulated prior guidance (FMPG), which tailors the guidance scale to low- and high-frequency bands coherent with bridge generative dynamics. To address prior exploitation in image in-painting, we develop a cascaded framework, CFG-FMPG, which first generates a noisy hidden representation via CFG and then exploits it as a generative prior with FMPG, fulfilling their complementary strengths without compromising inference efficiency. Experiments demonstrate that our PG methods consistently improve pre-trained bridge models across diverse image translation tasks.

</details>


### [31] [KC-3DGS: Kurtosis-Constrained Gaussian Splatting for High-Fidelity View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03120)
*Vivekjyoti Banerjee,Abhay Yadav,Rama Chellappa,Aniket Roy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KC-3DGS improves 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) for real-time novel view synthesis by adding wavelet-domain supervision to address oversmoothing and structural artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard pixel-space losses in 3DGS lead to oversmoothing and structural artifacts, particularly in sparse-view settings. The authors aim to improve perceptual quality by incorporating wavelet-domain supervision.

Method: KC-3DGS combines three components: (1) multi-scale wavelet coefficient alignment loss, (2) supervised kurtosis concentration loss, and (3) cross-band covariance penalty. This approach is a plug-and-play regularization strategy for existing 3DGS pipelines.

Result: Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements in perceptual quality across various datasets. KC-3DGS achieves a 9.48% improvement in DreamSim on the WRIVA-ULTRRA outdoor dataset and improves PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. In sparse-view settings, KC-3DGS improves PSNR by up to 0.5 dB on MipNeRF360.

Conclusion: The proposed KC-3DGS method effectively addresses the limitations of standard pixel-space losses in 3DGS, leading to improved perceptual quality and robustness in sparse-view settings.

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables real-time novel view synthesis by representing scenes as collections of anisotropic Gaussians optimized via differentiable rasterization. However, standard pixel-space losses (L1, SSIM) constrain only aggregate reconstruction error, permitting the optimization to redistribute error across frequency scales. This leads to oversmoothing and structural artifacts, particularly in sparse-view settings where supervision is limited. We propose KC-3DGS, which augments 3DGS training with wavelet-domain supervision based on natural image statistics. Our method combines three components: (1) a multi-scale wavelet coefficient alignment loss that explicitly penalizes missing high-frequency detail, (2) a supervised kurtosis concentration loss that encourages rendered images to match the heavy-tailed frequency statistics of ground-truth images, and (3) a cross-band covariance penalty that promotes frequency specialization. We provide theoretical analysis showing that pixel-space losses admit a family of indistinguishable perturbations under wavelet redistribution, and that our joint objective excludes degenerate solutions. Experiments across MipNeRF360, Tanks&Temples, MVImgNet, DeepBlending, and WRIVA-ULTRRA demonstrate consistent improvements in perceptual quality. On the challenging WRIVA-ULTRRA outdoor dataset, KC-3DGS achieves a 9.48% improvement in DreamSim while also improving PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. In sparse-view settings with only 12 training images, our method improves PSNR by up to 0.5 dB on MipNeRF360 while maintaining perceptual quality. The approach integrates seamlessly into existing 3DGS pipelines as a plug-and-play regularization strategy.

</details>


### [32] [Disentangling Visual and Factual Correctness in LVLMs' Visualization Literacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03142)
*Soohyun Lee,Jaeyoung Kim,Seokhyeon Park,Sihyeon Lee,Jiwon Song,Bohyoung Kim,Hyunjoo Song,Jinwook Seo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a framework to evaluate the visual reasoning capabilities of large vision-language models (LVLMs) and their reliance on factual priors versus visual evidence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current evaluations of LVLMs do not distinguish between genuine visual reasoning and factual knowledge learned during training, making it unclear whether their responses reflect actual visual understanding or memorized facts.

Method: The authors propose a new assessment test called Counterfactual Visualization Literacy Assessment Test (CVLAT) and conduct three experiments with 15 state-of-the-art LVLMs to evaluate their visual correctness and factual correctness.

Result: The results show that many LVLMs achieve high visualization accuracy but may rely on factual priors rather than visual evidence, and that prompt-based intervention can shift prioritization but its effectiveness varies across models.

Conclusion: The study highlights the importance of evaluating not only visualization literacy but also how models arbitrate between visual evidence and factual priors when the two diverge, and proposes a benchmark and code for future research.

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) show strong visualization interpretation, yet it is unclear whether their responses reflect genuine reasoning over visual evidence or factual priors learned during training. Current evaluations mix these two sources, obscuring when correct visual interpretation is overridden by memorized facts. We present a framework that isolates visual correctness from factual correctness, revealing validity limitations in existing visualization literacy assessments. Across three experiments with 15 state-of-the-art LVLMs: (1) several models reach human-level performance on standard tests (VLAT), but this may reflect factual recall rather than visual understanding, while randomized-data tests (reVLAT) underestimate literacy when correct visual interpretation is superseded by factual priors. (2) Using our Counterfactual Visualization Literacy Assessment Test (CVLAT) with capability-normalized arbitration metrics, we classify models by the sign of their visual-factual reliance index (VFRI), revealing a visualization-oriented majority and a factual knowledge-oriented minority, though several near-zero cases warrant caution. A human baseline (N=30) on the same counterfactual items confirms that people overwhelmingly follow the chart under conflict, providing a human reference point. (3) Prompt-based intervention can shift prioritization, but its effectiveness is highly model-dependent and direction-asymmetric, and high chart-reading capability does not predict prompt-controllability. Overall, high visualization accuracy is not sufficient evidence of faithful visual reasoning: reliable integration into visual analytics requires evaluating not only visualization literacy but also how models arbitrate between visual evidence and factual priors when the two diverge. Benchmark and code: https://github.com/JaeyoungKim-HCIL/CVLAT

</details>


### [33] [$A^2$: Smaller Self-Supervised ViTs Localize Better than Larger Ones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03148)
*Sreehari Rammohan,Huy Ha,Carl Vondrick*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a method called $A^2$ that combines the benefits of small and large Vision Transformers (ViTs) for robust visual classification. $A^2$ uses a small attention model to localize foreground objects and a large embedding model to extract rich representations, achieving competitive results with state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation behind this paper is to improve robust visual classification by effectively localizing foreground objects while ignoring contextual distractors. The authors observed that smaller self-supervised ViTs are better at localizing foreground objects, but larger ViTs extract richer representations.

Method: The proposed method, $A^2$, decouples the localization and representation extraction processes. It uses a small attention model to identify attention peaks, crops around these peaks, and then embeds the cropped regions with a larger model. This approach leverages pre-trained features, does not require group labels, and avoids per-dataset attention or backbone training.

Result: The $A^2$ method achieves competitive results with backbone-matched loss-level methods like DFR across 5 benchmarks. It also outperforms end-to-end attention training under stronger distribution shifts.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that $A^2$ offers a simple yet effective approach to robust visual classification by combining the benefits of small and large ViTs. This method provides a promising solution for improving the localization of foreground objects and extracting rich representations.

Abstract: Robust visual classification often depends on localizing the main foreground objects in an image while ignoring contextual distractors. Surprisingly, we find that the attention maps of smaller self-supervised ViTs localize foreground objects better than those of larger ViTs. However, we still need large ViTs, because they extract richer representations from each patch. To get the best of both worlds, good localization and rich representations, we propose $A^2$, a simple method that leverages this inverse scaling finding by decoupling where to look (a small attention model) from what to extract (a large embedding model): we crop around the attention peaks of a small model and embed the crops with a larger model. $A^2$ uses entirely pretrained features, requires no group labels, and does not require per-dataset attention or backbone training. Across 5 benchmarks, $A^2$ is competitive with backbone-matched loss-level methods like DFR, and outperforms end-to-end attention training under stronger distribution shifts.

</details>


### [34] [NVIDIA OmniDreams: Real-Time Generative World Model for Closed-Loop Autonomous Vehicle Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03159)
*NVIDIA,:,Aarti Basant,Amlan Kar,Despoina Paschalidou,Fangyin Wei,Francesco Ferroni,Guillermo Garcia Cobo,Haithem Turki,Huan Ling,Jaewoo Seo,James Lucas,Jay Zhangjie Wu,Jialiang Wang,Jonathan Lorraine,Jun Gao,Kai He,Katarina Tothova,Kevin Xie,Michał Tyszkiewicz,Qi Wu,Riccardo de Lutio,Ruilong Li,Sanja Fidler,Seung Wook Kim,Tianchang Shen,Tianshi Cao,Tobias Pfaff,William Lew,Xindi Wu,Xuanchi Ren,Yifan Lu,Yuxuan Zhang,Zan Gojcic,Zian Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OmniDreams, a generative world model, enables real-time simulation of complex driving scenarios, including extreme weather and dynamic agent behaviors, for safe evaluation of autonomous driving policies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The safe evaluation of driving policies in long-tail scenarios remains a critical bottleneck in autonomous vehicle development, and current simulators struggle to generalize to highly dynamic or novel scenes.

Method: OmniDreams is a foundation generative world model mid- and post-trained from the Cosmos diffusion model to autoregressively generate action-conditioned videos in real time, leveraging 21k hours of driving scenarios.

Result: OmniDreams enables scalable and comprehensive training and evaluation of next-generation autonomous driving policies, and a world-action model post-trained from OmniDreams achieves strong performance on the Physical AI Autonomous Vehicles NuRec dataset.

Conclusion: OmniDreams provides a highly responsive, reactive environment for training and evaluating autonomous driving policies, and has the potential to serve as a backbone for policy architectures.

Abstract: As autonomous vehicle capabilities advance, the safe evaluation of driving policies in long-tail scenarios remains a critical bottleneck. In closed-loop simulation, the driving policy model actively interacts with the environment, where its actions dynamically update the simulator state and directly influence the next set of generated sensor observations. While recent reconstruction-based neural simulators offer photorealism, they are fundamentally constrained by their initial captured data and struggle to generalize to highly dynamic or novel scenes. To overcome these limitations, we introduce OmniDreams, a foundation generative world model mid- and post-trained from the Cosmos diffusion model to autoregressively generate action-conditioned videos in real time. By leveraging the rich visual priors of Cosmos and mid- and post-training on 21k hours of driving scenarios, OmniDreams synthesizes complex, unobserved phenomena that are hard for traditional simulators to capture, such as extreme weather and unpredictable dynamic agent behaviors. Crucially, it autoregressively conditions its photorealistic sensor generation on past frames, the current simulator state, and immediate driving actions. Deployed in a closed-loop system with the Alpamayo 1 policy model and AlpaSim orchestrator, OmniDreams acts as a highly responsive, reactive environment, providing a scalable and comprehensive solution for training and evaluating next-generation autonomous driving policies. We additionally show preliminary results indicating that a world-action model (WAM) post-trained from OmniDreams achieves strong performance on the Physical AI Autonomous Vehicles NuRec dataset, surpassing the VLA-based Alpamayo 1.5 research policy model while using only 1/5 the total parameters. These results highlight the potential for a real-time world model like OmniDreams to also serve as a backbone for policy architectures.

</details>


### [35] [SRENet: Spectral Re-Entry Network for Point Cloud Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03160)
*Qiuxia Wu,Jiarui Lan,Wenxiong Kang,Zhiyong Wang,Kun Hu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes SRENet, a spectral-aware framework for recognizing human actions from point cloud sequences, achieving state-of-the-art performance on several datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The irregular structure and temporal inconsistency of point clouds pose unique challenges for spatio-temporal representation learning in action recognition.

Method: The proposed SRENet uses a Spectral Decomposition Block (SDeBlock) and a Spectral Re-entry Block (SReBlock) to learn global context and fine-grained temporal dynamics from a frequency perspective.

Result: SRENet achieves state-of-the-art performance on MSR-Action3D, NTU-RGBD, and NTU-RGBD120 datasets.

Conclusion: The paper validates the effectiveness of frequency modeling in point cloud-based action understanding, demonstrating that SRENet is a promising approach for 3D perception driven applications.

Abstract: Recognizing human actions from point cloud sequences is critical for 3D perception driven applications such as autonomous driving and human-computer interaction. However, the irregular structure and temporal inconsistency of point clouds pose unique challenges for spatio-temporal representation learning, especially in capturing both global motion context and fine-grained temporal dynamics. We propose SRENet, a spectral-aware framework designed to explicitly learn both global context and fine-grained temporal dynamics of motion from a frequency perspective for action recognition. SRENet introduces a Spectral Decomposition Block (SDeBlock) that performs wavelet-based analysis along temporal and spatial axes, disentangling features into low- and high-frequency components with frequency-specific attention. To recover residual dynamics and re-align temporal frequency structures distorted during semantic fusion, a Spectral Re-entry Block (SReBlock) performs secondary temporal decomposition. Furthermore, a spectral-aware learning strategy is devised to enhance discriminability in both frequency subspaces via contrastive loss and a curriculum schedule that gradually shifts focus from low- to high-frequency spaces in line with coarse to detailed motion patterns. Extensive experiments on MSR-Action3D, NTU-RGBD and NTU-RGBD120 demonstrate that SRENet achieves state-of-the-art performance, validating the effectiveness of frequency modeling in point cloud-based action understanding.

</details>


### [36] [JAVEDIT: Joint Audio-Visual Instruction-Guided Video Editing with Agentic Data Curation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03168)
*Yinan Chen,Chuming Lin,Zhennan Chen,Yuxiang Zeng,Junwei Zhu,Yali Bi,Xijie Huang,Chengming Xu,Donghao Luo,Zhucun Xue,Xiaobin Hu,Chengjie Wang,Yong Liu,Jiangning Zhang,Shuicheng Yan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new dataset and benchmark for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing is proposed, along with a baseline model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of dedicated datasets and benchmarks for joint audio-visual editing.

Method: Construction of JAVEdit-100k dataset and JAVEditBench benchmark, and proposal of JAVEdit baseline model.

Result: The JAVEdit model outperforms all baselines on five of six evaluation metrics.

Conclusion: The proposed dataset, benchmark, and model can facilitate research in instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing.

Abstract: While instruction-based video editing has seen significant progress, joint audio-visual editing remains constrained by the absence of dedicated datasets and benchmarks. To bridge this gap, we present JAVEdit-100k, the first large-scale, high-quality dataset tailored for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing. Focusing on human-centric videos, JAVEdit-100k comprises approximately 100K editing triplets spanning five distinct categories, including subject editing and speech editing. This dataset is rigorously constructed via four meticulously designed generation pipelines, seamlessly paired with an agent-in-the-loop quality control mechanism. Furthermore, to address the lack of standardized evaluation within the field, we introduce JAVEditBench, a comprehensive benchmark featuring curated source videos and human-aligned instructions across all editing categories. Finally, we propose JAVEdit, a pioneering baseline model for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing. Experiments show that \model\ outperforms all baselines on five of six evaluation metrics.

</details>


### [37] [Ask When It Pays: Cost-Aware Open-Ended Interaction for Instance Goal Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03175)
*Xunyi Zhao,Sihao Lin,Gengze Zhou,Zerui Li,Shijie Li,Wei Tao,Jiajun Liu,Qi Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a cost-sensitive approach to interactive Instance Goal Navigation (IGN), where an agent asks questions to disambiguate and find a specific object instance. It introduces a taxonomy of question types, a benchmark for evaluating interaction efficiency, and a zero-shot navigator that selectively queries based on expected uncertainty reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The ambiguity in natural-language descriptions for IGN cannot be resolved through perception and language alone, necessitating interaction with an oracle for disambiguation. Existing interactive methods treat all queries equally, not optimizing for efficient disambiguation.

Method: The paper recasts interactive IGN as a cost-sensitive uncertainty-reduction problem. It applies information-gain analysis on navigation corpora to identify effective cues and derive weights for question types. A benchmark and Weighted Success Rate metric are proposed to evaluate interaction efficiency.

Result: The proposed approach enables efficient disambiguation in IGN by selectively querying based on expected uncertainty reduction, justified by interaction cost. A zero-shot MLLM navigator is introduced for this purpose.

Conclusion: The paper presents a novel cost-sensitive approach to interactive IGN, optimizing for efficient disambiguation through selective querying. It establishes a framework for evaluating interaction efficiency and proposes a zero-shot navigator.

Abstract: Instance Goal Navigation (IGN) requires an embodied agent to find a specific object instance among distractors from an underspecified natural-language description. Such ambiguity often cannot be resolved from perception and language alone, making interaction with an oracle a natural mechanism for disambiguation. Prior interactive methods allow oracle queries but treat lightweight clarification and route-level guidance alike, letting agents boost success rate through repeated high-information questions rather than by resolving the underlying ambiguity efficiently. We recast interactive IGN as a cost-sensitive uncertainty-reduction problem, where the agent should ask the question whose answer provides the largest reduction in navigation uncertainty relative to its penalty. To this end, we apply an information-gain analysis on existing navigation corpora to identify which cues reduce navigation uncertainty, yielding a compact set of question types and data-derived weights.However, existing interactive navigation benchmarks do not model the cost of different question types or evaluate how efficiently agents use interaction, making them unsuitable for studying cost-sensitive interaction. Based on this taxonomy, we construct a benchmark for diagnosing interaction behavior and efficiency, together with a Weighted Success Rate metric that penalizes each query by its derived cost. We further propose a zero-shot MLLM navigator that selectively queries at each decision step only when the expected uncertainty reduction justifies the interaction cost.

</details>


### [38] [GLINT: Sparsely Gated Vision-Language Alignment for Fine-Grained Radiology Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03180)
*Jonggwon Park,Seongeun Lee,Junhyun Park,Hannah Yun,Hyunwoong Kim,Sohyun Jeong,Hyewon Kang,Byungmu Yoon,Kyoyun Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GLINT, a novel framework for vision-language models in radiology, enables zero-shot classification, grounding, and segmentation from free-text queries by modeling sparse correspondence between image and text.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation behind this paper is to address the challenge of mismatch in scale between image and report pairs in radiology, where supervision is provided only at the global level, not at the local level of specific findings in the image.

Method: The authors propose GLINT, which consists of two main components: Sparsely Gated Alignment and Dense Feature Regularization. The former uses a sigmoid gate to activate only relevant patches for a given textual query, while the latter anchors the trainable encoder's intermediate features to a frozen self-supervised learning teacher.

Result: GLINT outperforms existing methods on classification, report generation, and segmentation tasks, with notable gains in zero-shot grounding and segmentation, and is the first to demonstrate zero-shot segmentation on 3D CT volumes without mask supervision.

Conclusion: The proposed GLINT framework effectively addresses the challenge of sparse correspondence between image and text in radiology, enabling improved performance on various tasks, including zero-shot classification, grounding, and segmentation.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) for radiology have emerged as a scalable paradigm by leveraging image-report pairs naturally produced in clinical workflows. However, this pairing reveals a mismatch in scale: each finding occupies only a small region of the image, yet supervision is provided only at the global image-report level. This poses a central challenge: prior approaches spread weight densely across all patches rather than concentrating on the sparse subset relevant to a given query. To address this, we present GLINT (Gated Language-Image alignmeNT), a framework that explicitly models this sparse correspondence. On the alignment side, we introduce Sparsely Gated Alignment, a novel architecture in which a sigmoid gate over a separate gate embedding space activates only the patches relevant to each textual query, enforcing explicit sparsity. On the representation side, we add Dense Feature Regularization, which anchors the trainable encoder's intermediate features to a frozen self-supervised learning (SSL) teacher, preserving the fine-grained patch features that the gate relies on. The same recipe applies to both 2D chest X-ray (CXR) and 3D chest computed tomography (CT), built with DINOv3 and V-JEPA 2.1, respectively. GLINT enables zero-shot classification, grounding, and segmentation from free-text queries, and to our knowledge is the first to demonstrate zero-shot segmentation on 3D CT volumes without mask supervision. Notably, the most pronounced gains arise on zero-shot grounding and segmentation, where sparse, query-specific localization is required, consistent with our design intent. In downstream evaluation, GLINT outperforms both SSL encoders and medical VLMs on classification, report generation, and segmentation.

</details>


### [39] [Reinforcement Learning from Cross-domain Videos with Video Prediction Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03201)
*Zhao Yang,Xinrui Zu,Jacob E. Kooi,Thomas Delliaux,He Liu,Shujian Yu,Kevin Sebastian Luck,Vincent François-Lavet*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: XIPER, a reward model, enables reinforcement learning from expert videos across visually distinct domains by predicting video likelihood as a reward signal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge of learning from expert videos across domains with visual differences, such as color, morphology, or sim-to-real gaps, due to the absence of reward signals and domain gaps.

Method: XIPER trains a cross-domain video prediction model that maps agent observations into the expert domain and uses prediction likelihood as a reward signal.

Result: XIPER outperforms baselines on DMC Color Suite (8 tasks) and DMC Body Suite (3 tasks) despite domain gaps, and produces meaningful reward signals for real-robot observations given simulated expert videos.

Conclusion: XIPER offers a solution for reinforcement learning from expert videos across visually distinct domains, effectively addressing domain gaps and absence of reward signals.

Abstract: Reinforcement learning from expert videos across visually distinct domains is challenging due to the absence of reward signals and the presence of domain gaps. We introduce XIPER (Cross-domain Video Prediction Reward), a reward model for learning from expert videos collected in a visually different domain, where the agent's appearance differs due to factors such as color, morphology, or the sim-to-real gap. More specifically, XIPER trains a cross-domain video prediction model that maps agent observations into the expert domain and uses the prediction likelihood as a reward signal. Experiments on the DMC Color Suite (8 tasks) and DMC Body Suite (3 tasks) show that XIPER consistently outperforms baselines despite domain gaps such as differences in agent color and morphology. We further analyze XIPER on a sim-to-real transfer dataset, demonstrating that it produces meaningful reward signals for real-robot observations given only simulated expert videos. Code, pretrained models, datasets and video demonstrations can be found on our project webpage: https://sites.google.com/view/xiper

</details>


### [40] [Follow-Your-Preference++: Rethinking Preference Alignment for Image Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03216)
*Junkun Yuan,Yutao Shen,Toru Aonishi,Hideki Nakayama,Yue Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper studies preference alignment for image inpainting using direct preference optimization and publicly available reward models, finding that ensemble methods mitigate biases and improve performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the core challenges of preference alignment for image inpainting and improve performance without modifying model architectures or introducing additional datasets.

Method: The authors adopt the direct preference optimization framework, construct preference training data with publicly available reward models, and evaluate across nine reward models, two benchmarks, and two baseline inpainting models.

Result: The study finds that most reward models provide valid signals, but display biases that can induce reward hacking; ensemble methods mitigate these biases and yield robust performance, substantially outperforming prior state-of-the-art models.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that preference alignment can be improved through ensemble methods, and that these improvements are transferable to other tasks, such as object removal.

Abstract: We study preference alignment for image inpainting. Rather than proposing yet another method, we revisit the problem from first principles and reassess its core challenges. We adopt the widely used direct preference optimization framework and construct preference training data with publicly available reward models. Our empirical study spans nine reward models, two benchmarks, and two baseline inpainting models that differ in architecture and generative mechanism. Our main findings are: (1) Most reward models provide valid signals for preference data construction, although some are unreliable as evaluators. (2) Across models and benchmarks, preference data exhibits consistent trends under both candidate and sample scaling. (3) Reward models display pronounced biases--particularly in brightness, composition, and color scheme--that make them prone to inducing reward hacking. (4) A simple ensemble of reward models mitigates such biases and yields robust, generalizable performance. {\color{rebuttal_blue}(5) Preference alignment is transferable to the object removal task, where the goal shifts from open-ended creative generation to coherent background completion. (6) Further analysis reveals that a calibrated ensemble method further mitigates hacking and improves robustness.} Without modifying model architectures or introducing additional datasets, our models substantially outperform prior state-of-the-art models on standard metrics, large vision-language model evaluations, and human assessments. Our code is available at: https://github.com/shenytzzz/Follow-Your-Preference.

</details>


### [41] [MemoGen: Can Past Experience Improve Future Text-to-Image Generation?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03243)
*Wenshuo Chen,Kuimou Yu,Bowen Tian,Jianfei Song,Shaofeng Liang,Haozhe Jia,Kan Cheng,Haosen Li,Kaishen Yuan,Lei Wang,Jiemin Wu,Songning Lai,Yutao Yue*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes MemoGen, a training-free framework that enables text-to-image models to continually improve from their own generation experience without updating the underlying generator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing text-to-image models struggle with implicit visual constraints, relational reasoning, and external knowledge. Current retrieval-augmented and agentic generation methods treat each generation as an isolated episode and do not preserve past successes or failures for future use.

Method: The authors propose MemoGen, which augments existing image generators with an agentic evolution layer. This layer infers visual requirements, retrieves external evidence and references, translates them into generation constraints, evaluates generated results, and stores task understanding and experience as reusable memory.

Result: Experiments on knowledge-intensive and reasoning-oriented benchmarks show that MemoGen surpasses strong proprietary systems after only two evolution rounds, demonstrating the effectiveness of explicit experience memory for reliable text-to-image generation.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that explicit experience memory can serve as a powerful continual learning signal for reliable text-to-image generation, enabling test-time self-evolution without parameter updates.

Abstract: Modern text-to-image models have achieved strong visual synthesis, yet remain unreliable when prompts require implicit visual constraints, relational reasoning, or external knowledge. Existing retrieval-augmented and agentic generation methods mitigate this issue by acquiring external knowledge, references, or refined prompts for the current request, yet they typically treat each generation as an isolated episode and do not systematically preserve past successes or failures for future use. In this work, we ask whether a text-to-image system can continually improve from its own generation experience without updating the underlying generator. We propose MemoGen, a training-free framework that augments existing image generators with an agentic evolution layer. For each task, MemoGen explicitly infers visual requirements, retrieves external evidence and references when necessary, translates them into executable generation constraints, evaluates the generated result, and stores task understanding, reference choices, visual feedback, successful strategies, and failure lessons as reusable experience memory. Across evolution rounds, the agent retrieves relevant experience to improve similar future generations, selectively repairing previously failed cases while preserving successful ones, thereby enabling test-time self-evolution without parameter updates. Extensive experiments on knowledge-intensive and reasoning-oriented benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of this paradigm: after only two evolution rounds, MemoGen built upon the open-source Qwen-Image backbone surpasses strong proprietary systems such as Nano Banana Pro and GPT-Image-1 on WISE and Mind-Bench, showing that explicit experience memory can serve as a powerful continual learning signal for reliable text-to-image generation.

</details>


### [42] [MariData: One-Step Unpaired Image Translation for Maritime Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03246)
*Santeri Henriksson,Mehdi Asadi,Amin Majd,Juha Kalliovaara*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a method for generating synthetic maritime data using CycleGAN-turbo to address the scarcity of diverse training data for Maritime Autonomous Surface Ships (MASS) in adverse weather and low-light conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The development of robust perception systems for MASS is constrained by the scarcity of diverse training data, particularly for adverse weather and low-light conditions. Collecting paired images in dynamic maritime environments is physically impossible.

Method: The authors introduce a framework for generating synthetic maritime data using CycleGAN-turbo, a one-step unpaired translation architecture. They incorporate zero-convolution skip connections to bypass the Variational Autoencoder (VAE) bottleneck, preserving small object details during translation.

Result: The method effectively synthesizes realistic atmospheric conditions while maintaining the underlying semantic structure of the scene. Qualitative evaluations and variable-strength inference studies demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.

Conclusion: The work establishes an efficient, structure-aware data synthesis pipeline that directly addresses the data scarcity bottleneck in autonomous maritime navigation.

Abstract: The development on robust perception systems for Maritime Autonomous Surface Ships (MASS) is heavily constrained by the scarcity of diverse training data, particularly for adverse weather and low-light conditions. Because collecting paired images in dynamic maritime environments is physically impossible, synthetic data generation via unpaired image-to-image translation offers a critical solution. However, existing generative models suffer from failing to preserve the fine structural details of small navigational objects due to latent compression bottlenecks. In this paper, we introduce a framework for generating synthetic maritime data using CycleGAN-turbo, a one-step unpaired translation architecture. By incorporating zero-convolution skip connections to bypass the Variational Autoencoder (VAE) bottleneck, our approach explicitly preserves small object details (e.g., distant vessels and sea marks) during translation. We compiled a dataset of 7,000 maritime images to train and evaluate models for Day-to-Foggy, Day-to-Sunset, and Day-to-Night domain translations. Qualitative evaluations and variable-strength inference studies demonstrate that our method effectively synthesizes realistic atmospheric conditions while maintaining the underlying semantic structure of the scene. The Day-to-Foggy and Day-to-Sunset models exhibit great structural retention, whereas the Day-to-Night model highlights the challenge of semantic hallucination, such as generating artificial coastal lights, induced by unbalanced training distributions. Ultimately, this work establishes an efficient, structure-aware data synthesis pipeline that directly addresses the data scarcity bottleneck in autonomous maritime navigation.

</details>


### [43] [FreeStreamGS: Online Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting from Unposed Streaming Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03254)
*Ruiyang Chen,Feiran Li,Chu Zhou,Zonglin Li,Zhanyu Ma,Heng Guo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposed FreeStreamGS, a framework for online novel view synthesis from streaming unposed images, achieving high-quality rendering competitive with offline methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting methods can't handle online novel view synthesis from streaming and unposed image inputs due to severe rendering artifacts.

Method: Introduced FreeStreamGS with two key mechanisms: Decoupled Intrinsic Recovery Head and Dynamic Point Refinement Offset strategy.

Result: FreeStreamGS achieved rendering quality competitive with state-of-the-art offline feed-forward 3DGS methods.

Conclusion: FreeStreamGS enables efficient and high-quality online novel view synthesis from streaming and unposed image inputs.

Abstract: Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) allows efficient and high-fidelity novel view synthesis (NVS) from an offline recorded image sequence. However, achieving online NVS from streaming and unposed image inputs remains challenging. Although online feed-forward geometric estimation methods have been proposed for streaming depth and point cloud recovery, they cannot be adapted to NVS due to severe rendering artifacts. This is because NVS demands stricter multi-view consistency in Gaussian scales and pose-geometry alignment; even minor deviations would accumulate over time and visibly degrade rendering quality. To this end, we propose FreeStreamGS, a robust online feed-forward framework for efficient and high-quality NVS. We introduce two key mechanisms: a Decoupled Intrinsic Recovery Head that removes cumulative camera intrinsic bias and prevents scene scale jitter during long-term streaming, and a Dynamic Point Refinement Offset strategy that relaxes rigid unprojection to correct coupled pose-depth drift. Extensive experiments show that FreeStreamGS achieves rendering quality competitive with state-of-the-art offline feed-forward 3DGS methods, despite operating without access to future frames.

</details>


### [44] [PaddleOCR-VL-1.6: Expanding the Frontier of Document Parsing with Under-Optimized Region Refinement and Progressive Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03264)
*Zelun Zhang,Hongen Liu,Suyin Liang,Yubo Zhang,Yiqing Xiang,Jiaxuan Liu,Ting Sun,Manhui Lin,Yue Zhang,Changda Zhou,Tingquan Gao,Cheng Cui,Yi Liu,Dianhai Yu,Yanjun Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PaddleOCR-VL-1.6, an upgraded document parsing model, achieves a state-of-the-art score of 96.33% on OmniDocBench v1.6 through targeted data optimization and progressive post-training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The previous model, PaddleOCR-VL-1.5, had remaining errors in under-optimized regions, and the new model aims to improve these areas.

Method: PaddleOCR-VL-1.6 introduces a region-aware data optimization framework and a progressive post-training recipe based on curated data selection and reinforcement learning.

Result: PaddleOCR-VL-1.6 achieves a new state-of-the-art score of 96.33% on OmniDocBench v1.6 and demonstrates strong competitiveness against top-tier VLMs.

Conclusion: PaddleOCR-VL-1.6 provides a practical post-training recipe for the PaddleOCR-VL series and achieves significant performance improvement.

Abstract: We introduce PaddleOCR-VL-1.6, an upgraded compact document parsing model built upon PaddleOCR-VL-1.5. Although PaddleOCR-VL-1.5 establishes a strong 0.9B baseline, its remaining errors concentrate in under-optimized regions where model behavior is unstable, data coverage is sparse, or supervision is unreliable. Rather than expanding the training corpus indiscriminately, PaddleOCR-VL-1.6 introduces a region-aware data optimization framework that identifies weak regions from the previous model, applies targeted enhancement to these regions, and improves the reliability of supervision signals. It further adopts a progressive post-training recipe based on curated data selection and reinforcement learning, pushing model performance to a higher level through staged optimization. PaddleOCR-VL-1.6 achieves a new state-of-the-art score of 96.33% on OmniDocBench v1.6, demonstrates strong competitiveness against top-tier VLMs, and provides a practical post-training recipe for the PaddleOCR-VL series.

</details>


### [45] [VistaHop: Benchmarking Multi-hop Visual Reasoning for Visual DeepSearch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03273)
*Hang He,Chuhuai Yue,Chengqi Dong,Chengcheng Wan,Ting Su,Haiying Sun,Jiajun Chai,Xiaohan Wang,Guojun Yin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduced VistaHop, a benchmark for evaluating vision-centric search and multi-hop visual reasoning in Visual DeepSearch, and VistaArena, a unified evaluation environment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks have limitations in evaluating iterative image inspection, visual-anchor grounding, and multi-hop evidence integration in Visual DeepSearch.

Method: Introduced VistaHop benchmark with 300 high-resolution images, 25 visual search scenarios, and 350 multi-hop QA tasks, and VistaArena evaluation environment.

Result: The best model, SenseNova-MARS-32B, achieved only 24.31% Pass@1, revealing limitations in visual grounding, evidence revisiting, long-chain reasoning, and multi-anchor information fusion.

Conclusion: Current MLRMs have persistent limitations in Visual DeepSearch, highlighting the need for stronger benchmarks and training methods.

Abstract: Visual DeepSearch requires multimodal large reasoning model (MLRM) agents to answer complex visual queries by repeatedly inspecting image regions, grounding intermediate reasoning in visual evidence, and connecting fine-grained clues across long reasoning chains. However, existing benchmarks mainly focus on single-step visual understanding or static image-question answering, offering limited evaluation of iterative image inspection, visual-anchor grounding, and multi-hop evidence integration. In this work, we introduce VistaHop, a benchmark for evaluating vision-centric search and multi-hop visual reasoning in Visual DeepSearch. VistaHop contains 300 high-resolution images, 25 visual search scenarios, and 350 multi-hop QA tasks that require models to follow evidence chains from visual anchors or fuse information across multiple image-grounded reasoning paths. We further develop VistaArena, a unified evaluation environment that supports tool-augmented reasoning with text search, image search, image cropping, and evidence-based answer validation. Experiments on seven representative MLRMs show that current models remain far from solving VistaHop: the best model, SenseNova-MARS-32B, achieves only 24.31% Pass@1. These results reveal persistent limitations in visual grounding, evidence revisiting, long-chain reasoning, and multi-anchor information fusion, highlighting the need for stronger benchmarks and training methods for Visual DeepSearch.

</details>


### [46] [BA-T: An Iterative Transformer for Two-View Bundle Adjustment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03287)
*Ganlin Zhang,Weirong Chen,Daniel Cremers,Xi Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The authors propose BA-T, a lightweight iterative Transformer for 3D reconstruction that achieves strong performance and cross-view consistency with fewer parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing feed-forward models for 3D reconstruction rely on heavy decoder stacks and lack structured geometry refinement, leading to poor multi-view consistency.

Method: The authors propose BA-T, an iterative Transformer that implements BA-style structured updates as a repeatable layer in implicit token space, refining predictions based on latent residual by a single lightweight layer.

Result: BA-T progressively improves pose and reconstruction accuracy across iterations, achieves stronger cross-view consistency, and matches or surpasses larger models while using only 16% of their decoder parameters.

Conclusion: BA-T provides a compact, efficient, and structural alternative to depth-heavy attention, enabling accurate 3D reconstruction within a lightweight architecture.

Abstract: Feed-forward models for 3D reconstruction have achieved strong performance using deep cross-view attention to exchange information across images. However, these approaches often depend on heavy decoder stacks and lack a structured mechanism for geometry refinement, resulting in poor multi-view consistency. We address this by drawing inspiration from classical bundle adjustment (BA), which can be viewed as an iterative information propagation process between poses and local geometry. Inspired by BA, we propose BA-T, an iterative Transformer that implements BA-style structured updates as a repeatable layer in implicit token space. Instead of relying on deep attention stacks, BA-T refines predictions based on latent residual by a single lightweight layer. Experiments demonstrate that BA-T progressively improves pose and reconstruction accuracy across iterations, achieves stronger cross-view consistency than conventional decoders, and matches or surpasses substantially larger models while using only 16% of their decoder parameters. BA-T provides a compact, efficient, and structural alternative to depth-heavy attention, enabling accurate 3D reconstruction within a lightweight architecture. The code will be made publicly at https://github.com/zhangganlin/BA-T.

</details>


### [47] [TASE: Truncation-Aware Semantic Embeddings for 3D Scene Understanding and Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03314)
*Tim-Felix Faasch,Jochen Kall,Lucas Nunes,Jens Behley,Cyrill Stachniss*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TASE enables flexible 3D scene editing by projecting 2D semantic features into a truncation-aware embedding space, allowing text-driven edits with explicit control over modifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current 3D scene editing methods have limited support for controllable editing, which is crucial for applications like robotics, autonomous driving, and simulation.

Method: TASE projects pretrained 2D semantic features into a truncation-aware embedding space, optimizing a feature space with progressively reducing feature channels for abstract semantic representations and using a scale- and translation-equivariance loss for multi-view consistency.

Result: TASE demonstrates competitive performance in 3D scene editing, outperforming prior methods on edits involving large geometric modifications.

Conclusion: TASE enables flexible and controllable 3D scene editing with text-driven edits, providing explicit control over modifications and allowing more substantial changes than prior methods.

Abstract: High-fidelity semantic 3D scene representations are crucial for numerous applications, including robotics, autonomous driving, and simulation. Beyond this, the ability to edit such representations enables developers to adapt these applications more easily to specific target scenarios. Current approaches provide limited support for controllable editing. We introduce TASE, a method that projects pretrained 2D semantic features into a truncation-aware embedding space to enable flexible 3D scene editing. Our method explicitly optimizes a feature space in which progressively reducing feature channels yields increasingly abstract semantic representations, while retaining more channels preserves fine-grained detail. Additionally, we improve multi-view consistency of the features using a scale- and translation-equivariance loss. The resulting truncation-aware embedding space enables text-driven edits to 3D scenes, providing explicit control over how strongly edits adhere to the original scene content and allowing more substantial modifications than prior methods. Moreover, we propose a finetuning stage for the editing diffusion model to mitigate artifacts caused by geometric changes. Experimental results demonstrate competitive performance in 3D scene editing, substantially outperforming prior methods on edits involving large geometric modifications.

</details>


### [48] [Cross-Modality Feature Fusion Based on Structured State Space Duality for Multimodal Image Registration Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03341)
*Zhikang Li,Yan Wu,Xin Hu,Yi Dai,Ming Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel algorithm, RegNetMamba-2, is proposed for multi-modal image registration, leveraging Structured State Space Duality (SSD) to effectively extract local and global structural features, achieving good performance and efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The primary challenge in multi-modal image registration is shared structural information extraction. SSD offers greater global structural feature extraction with higher efficiency compared to Transformers.

Method: RegNetMamba-2 incorporates SSD into a coarse-to-fine matching process, applying SSD in three different scales for multi-modal feature extraction, and proposing a cross-modality feature fusion model with CMI and MSF modules.

Result: RegNetMamba-2 achieved good effects in both performance and efficiency for multi-modal image registration on VIS-SAR, VIS-IR, and VIS-NIR datasets, compared to state-of-the-art deep-learning based algorithms.

Conclusion: The proposed RegNetMamba-2 algorithm demonstrates effectiveness in multi-modal image registration, leveraging the advantages of SSD for efficient and accurate feature extraction.

Abstract: In multi-modal image registration, the primary challenge lies in shared structural information extraction. Compared to Transformers, Structured State Space Duality (SSD) offers greater global structural feature extraction with higher efficiency during training and inference. Inspired by these advantages, we propose a novel algorithm for multi-modal image registration, named RegNetMamba-2. Our algorithm incorporates SSD into coarse-to-fine matching process to extract local and global structural features effectively. Firstly, SSD is applied in three different scales for multi-modal feature extraction in our network. To strengthen local representation, we pay more attention on foreground edge and structural information by feature scaling function of SSD. Secondly, for shared feature extraction of input images and multi-modal feature fusion in all scales, we propose cross-modality feature fusion model based on SSD, consisting of Cross-Modality feature Interaction (CMI) module and Multi-Scale feature Fusion (MSF) module. CMI module is designed for cross-modality feature extraction of each scale by SSD in cross form. MSF module is designed to employ a progressive upward fusion in feature-level to obtain fine features, consisting of multi-modal features in all scales. Following coarse-to-fine, the features in 1/8 scale from CMI and 1/2 scale from MSF are collected to calculate matching probability scores. Then we respectively establish matching process by correspondences of pixel-wise. Extensive experiments demonstrate that comparing with state-of-the-art deep-learning based algorithms, RegNetMamba-2 has achieved good effects in both performance and efficiency for multi-modal image registration on the following datasets: VIS-SAR (OSDataset), VIS-IR (LGHD/RoadSence) and VIS-NIR (RGB-NIR sense).

</details>


### [49] [Beyond Semantics: Modeling Factual and Affective Perceptual Experiences from Vision-Language Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03345)
*Youssef Mohamed,Kenneth Ward Church,Mohamed Elhoseiny*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel approach called P-Topics modeling is introduced to understand how images are perceived affectively and across cultures. A two-stage architecture called PercepT is proposed, which discovers visual-textual clusters and associates images to their relevant perception experiences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The goal is to discover and model different perception experiences in a dataset of images and captions, and associate images to their relevant perception experiences.

Method: PercepT, a two-stage architecture, is introduced. It first discovers P-Topics as visual-textual clusters using an unsupervised training objective, and then learns P-Topic mapping functions via attention pooling to associate images to their respective clusters.

Result: PercepT achieves a silhouette score of 0.97 and an AUC score of 0.94, outperforming existing methods. Human evaluation confirms that PercepT captures semantically meaningful perception experiences.

Conclusion: PercepT effectively captures perception experiences and significantly outperforms existing methods, providing a novel approach for understanding how images are perceived affectively and across cultures.

Abstract: We present P-Topics (Perception Topics) modeling, a novel problem for understanding how images are perceived affectively and across cultures. The goal is to (1) discover and model the different perception experiences in a dataset of images and captions, where each experience is defined by an objective factual and a subjective affective aspect, and (2) associate images to their relevant perception experiences. We introduce **PercepT** (**Percep**tion topic **T**ransformer), a two-stage architecture that tackles P-Topics modeling. In the formation stage, percepT discovers *P-Topics* as visual-textual clusters using an unsupervised training objective, and dynamically selects the number of clusters to match the perceptual richness of the dataset. In the mapping stage, it learns *P-Topic mapping functions* via attention pooling to associate images to their respective clusters. On ArtELingo, PercepT achieves a silhouette score of **0.97** compared to **0.37** from the closest baseline reflecting better perceptual clusters. PercepT also achieves an AUC score of **0.94** compared to **0.77** showing better mapping to perceptual clusters. Human evaluation confirms that PercepT captures semantically meaningful perception experiences and significantly outperforms existing methods. Our implementation will be made public.

</details>


### [50] [SynCred-Bench: Benchmarking Synthetic Credibility in AI-Generated Visual Misinformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03348)
*Junxiao Yang,Minghao Zhang,Xiaoce Wang,Haoran Liu,Shiyao Cui,Hongning Wang,Minlie Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new benchmark for detecting AI-generated misinformation images with embedded text and layouts, and evaluates existing systems, finding them to be unreliable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The rise of generative models producing realistic visual artifacts with embedded text and layouts creates a new threat of synthetic credibility, making it difficult to distinguish between real and fake images.

Method: The authors introduce SYNCRED-Bench, a benchmark of 600 AI-generated misinformation images, and FP450, a real-image negative set, to evaluate the performance of existing systems in detecting synthetic credibility.

Result: Existing systems, including MLLMs, open-source AIGC detectors, and commercial APIs, show poor performance in detecting synthetic credibility, with true positive rates ranging from 10.5% to 57.6%, and human annotators achieving only 63% TPR.

Conclusion: The findings highlight synthetic credibility as a severe and underexplored visual misinformation challenge, and provide a benchmark for developing detectors that can reason beyond superficial credibility cues.

Abstract: Recent generative models can now produce visual artifacts with realistic embedded text and layouts, creating a new misinformation threat: synthetic credibility. We introduce SYNCRED-Bench, a benchmark of 600 AI-generated misinformation images balanced across six credible-form categories and seven fine-grained circulation styles, together with FP450, a real-image negative set for measuring false positives. Extensive evaluation shows that existing systems remain unreliable: under a 5% false-positive-rate constraint, 15 MLLMs achieve only 10.5% true positive rate (TPR), open-source AIGC detectors achieve less than 5%, and commercial APIs reach 57.6%. Human annotators also struggled to identify synthetic credibility, reaching only 63% TPR. These findings establish synthetic credibility as a severe and underexplored visual misinformation challenge, and provide a benchmark for developing detectors that reason beyond superficial credibility cues.

</details>


### [51] [P\textsuperscript{2}-DPO: Grounding Hallucination in Perceptual Processing via Calibration Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03376)
*Ruipeng Zhang,Zhihao Li,Haozhang Yuan,C. L. Philip Chen,Tong Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel training paradigm called P2-DPO is proposed to address the hallucination issue in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) by generating and learning from its own preference pairs, targeting perceptual bottlenecks and improving visual robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address the limitations of existing Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) methods, which do not specifically target perceptual bottlenecks in attended regions or address insufficient visual robustness against image degradation.

Method: The method proposed is Perceptual Processing Direct Preference Optimization (P2-DPO), which includes an on-policy preference pairs construction method and a Calibration Loss to align visual signals with text generation.

Result: Experimental results show that P2-DPO outperforms strong baselines that rely on costly human feedback on benchmarks, and evaluations validate its effectiveness in addressing perceptual bottlenecks and improving visual robustness.

Conclusion: The proposed P2-DPO paradigm offers a novel approach to addressing hallucination issues in LVLMs by directly targeting visual bottlenecks and improving visual robustness, with comparable performance to human-feedback-based methods but at a lower cost.

Abstract: Hallucination has recently garnered significant research attention in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) aims to learn directly from the corrected preferences provided by humans, thereby addressing the hallucination issue. Despite its success, this paradigm has yet to specifically target the perceptual bottleneck in attended regions or address insufficient Visual Robustness against image degradation. Furthermore, existing preference pairs are often vision-agnostic and their inherently off-policy nature limits their effectiveness in guiding model learning. To address these challenges, we propose Perceptual Processing Direct Preference Optimization (P\textsuperscript{2}-DPO), a novel training paradigm in which the model generates and learns from its own preference pairs, thereby directly addressing the identified visual bottlenecks while inherently avoiding the issues of vision-agnostic and off-policy data. It introduces: (1) an on-policy preference pairs construction method targeting Focus-and-Enhance perception and Visual Robustness, and (2) a well-designed Calibration Loss to precisely align visual signals with the causal generation of text. Experimental results demonstrate that with a comparable amount of training data and cost, P\textsuperscript{2}-DPO outperforms strong baselines that rely on costly human feedback on benchmarks. Furthermore, evaluations on Attention Region Fidelity (ARF) and image degradation scenarios validate the effectiveness of P\textsuperscript{2}-DPO in addressing perceptual bottleneck in attended regions and improving Visual Robustness against degraded inputs.

</details>


### [52] [Towards Characterizing Scientific Image Utility and Upgradability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03401)
*WenZhe Li,Qihang Yan,Liang Chen,Junying Wang,Farong Wen,Yijin Guo,Chunyi Li,Zicheng Zhang,Guangtao Zhai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new framework called SIU²A is proposed to evaluate the integrity of scientific images, addressing the limitations of existing methods in detecting errors and ensuring scientific validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The integrity of scientific images is threatened by AI-generated content with subtle but consequential errors, and existing evaluation paradigms are inadequate.

Method: The SIU²A framework introduces two dimensions: Utility (error detection and correction feasibility) and Upgradability (quality of correction), and implements a two-stage evaluation protocol.

Result: Experiments show that current multimodal systems have significant limitations in scientific error assessment and faithful correction, revealing a gap between visual perception and scientific usability.

Conclusion: The proposed SIU²A framework and benchmark dataset can help address the challenges in evaluating scientific image integrity and promoting trustworthy research communication.

Abstract: Scientific images function as critical evidence in research communication, yet their integrity faces unprecedented threats from AI-generated content that introduces subtle but consequential errors. Existing evaluation paradigms prove inadequate: perceptual quality metrics poorly correlate with scientific validity, while language models lack domain-specific verification capabilities. To address this gap, we propose the \textbf{S}cientific \textbf{I}mage \textbf{U}tility and \textbf{U}pgradability \textbf{A}ssessment (\textbf{SIU$^2$A}) framework, which introduces two complementary dimensions for scientific image evaluation. \textbf{Utility} encompasses \textit{error detection} (identifying scientific inaccuracies) and \textit{correction feasibility} (assessing whether errors can be reliably repaired). \textbf{Upgradability} measures the quality of correction. We categorize scientific image corruption into four fundamental types: Detail Distortion, Incompleteness, False Content, and Entity Confusion. Based on this taxonomy, we construct SIU$^2$A-Benchmark, a dataset with expert annotations for error identification and repair. The framework implements a two-stage evaluation protocol: the \textit{Utility} stage evaluates error detection capability and repair instruction generation, while the \textit{Upgradability} stage assesses whether corrections faithfully restore scientific validity without compromising existing accurate information. Experiments reveal that current multimodal systems exhibit significant limitations in both scientific error assessment and faithful correction, exposing a fundamental gap between visual perception and scientific usability.

</details>


### [53] [Mamba-Enhanced Implicit Motion Learning for Audio-Driven Portrait Animation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03402)
*Xuan Wei,Jiahui Chen,Kaiheng Li,Mingyu Shao,Qingqi Hong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel implicit-motion framework is proposed for generating realistic and temporally coherent human motion videos from a single static image and audio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional keypoint-based methods struggle to capture subtle motion dynamics, and the authors aim to improve the accuracy, naturalness, and temporal coherence of human motion video generation.

Method: A two-stage pipeline that decouples motion prediction from rendering, using a region-aware attention mechanism and a Mamba-enhanced diffusion model.

Result: The proposed method outperforms prior work across multiple public benchmarks and a collected dataset in accuracy, naturalness, and temporal coherence.

Conclusion: The proposed implicit-motion framework sets a new state-of-the-art in generating realistic and temporally coherent human motion videos from a single static image and audio.

Abstract: Audio-driven human motion video generation aims to synthesize realistic and temporally coherent human animations from a single static image, with applications in talking-head synthesis, co-speech gesture generation, and dynamic presentations. Moving beyond conventional keypoint-based methods that often struggle to capture subtle motion dynamics, We propose a novel implicit-motion framework for generating realistic and temporally coherent human motion videos from a single static image and audio. Our approach uses a two-stage pipeline that decouples motion prediction from rendering. The first stage integrates appearance priors and hierarchical depth cues into a region-aware attention mechanism to model latent motion features. The second stage employs a Mamba-enhanced diffusion model to directly predict these features from audio and the source image, enabling unsupervised learning of fine-grained motion patterns. This decoupled architecture enhances flexibility and efficiency. Trained on a new 380-hour high-quality dataset, our method outperforms prior work across multiple public benchmarks and our collected data in accuracy, naturalness, and temporal coherence, setting a new state-of-the-art.

</details>


### [54] [SAMatcher: Co-Visibility Modeling with Segment Anything for Robust Feature Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03406)
*Xu Pan,Qiyuan Ma,Mingyue Dong,He Chen,Wei Ji,Xianwei Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes SAMatcher, a feature matching framework that uses co-visibility modeling to improve correspondence estimation in image processing, leveraging the Segment Anything Model (SAM) and achieving substantial improvements over existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation behind this paper is to address the limitations of existing learning-based methods for correspondence estimation, which often operate at the pixel or patch level and lack explicit modeling of jointly visible regions across views.

Method: The method proposed in this paper is SAMatcher, a feature matching framework that formulates correspondence estimation through co-visibility modeling, using a symmetric cross-view interaction mechanism and a unified supervision scheme.

Result: The results of the paper demonstrate substantial improvements over existing matching pipelines, particularly under large viewpoint and scale variations, showing that foundation models can be effectively extended to multi-view correspondence reasoning through explicit co-visibility modeling.

Conclusion: The conclusion of this paper is that SAMatcher offers a new perspective on structured representation learning for image matching, and that foundation models originally designed for monocular segmentation can be effectively extended to multi-view correspondence reasoning.

Abstract: Reliable correspondence estimation is a fundamental problem in image processing, underpinning applications such as Structure from Motion, visual localization, and image registration. Existing learning-based methods have significantly improved local feature representations, yet most still operate at the pixel or patch level and lack explicit modeling of regions that are jointly visible across views. We propose SAMatcher, a feature matching framework that formulates correspondence estimation through co-visibility modeling. Instead of directly matching local features, SAMatcher first predicts co-visible region masks and bounding boxes as structured priors for correspondence estimation. Built upon the Segment Anything Model (SAM), it introduces a symmetric cross-view interaction mechanism that enables bidirectional feature exchange and cross-view semantic alignment. We further develop a unified supervision scheme that jointly optimizes mask prediction and box localization through mask learning, box regression, and mask-box consistency constraints. Extensive experiments on challenging benchmarks demonstrate substantial improvements over existing matching pipelines, particularly under large viewpoint and scale variations. Our results show that foundation models originally designed for monocular segmentation can be effectively extended to multi-view correspondence reasoning through explicit co-visibility modeling, offering a new perspective on structured representation learning for image matching. Code and project page: https://xupan.top/Projects/samatcher

</details>


### [55] [Enginuity: A Dataset and Benchmark for Vision-Language Understanding of Engineering Diagrams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03410)
*Abhishek Kumar,Isha Motiyani,Tilak Kasturi,Ethan Seefried,Prahitha Movva,Tirthankar Ghosal*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces Enginuity, a dataset and benchmark for evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) on complex engineering diagrams, and assesses the performance of four VLMs on two tasks: parts-table extraction and visual diagram question answering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is a lack of a public benchmark for measuring VLM capabilities on engineering diagrams, which are crucial for service, repair, and design workflows.

Method: The authors introduce Enginuity, a dataset and benchmark for evaluating VLMs on complex engineering diagrams, and evaluate four frontier VLMs (GPT-5.2 Chat, Claude Opus 4.7, Gemma 4, Qwen3-VL-32B-Instruct) under zero-shot and chain-of-thought prompting on two tasks: structured parts-table extraction and free-form visual diagram question answering.

Result: The models show varying performance on the two tasks, with Recall@all of 0.61-0.87 but Token F1pen of only 0.03-0.18 on Task 1, and a consistent factual-reasoning gap across all models on Task 2.

Conclusion: The paper highlights the challenges of evaluating VLMs on engineering diagrams and the need for domain-specific evaluation, and releases the dataset, annotations, evaluation harness, and per-sample model outputs to support a reproducible study of VLM capability on engineering content.

Abstract: Engineering diagrams pose a distinct challenge for vision-language models: unlike natural images or general documents, they encode information through dense spatial layouts, domain-specific symbols, and cross-references between visual callouts and structured parts tables. Despite their centrality to service, repair, and design workflows, there is no public benchmark for measuring VLM capabilities in this domain; existing datasets primarily focus on flowcharts, scientific figures, or business documents. To address this gap, we introduce Enginuity, the first open dataset and benchmark for evaluating VLMs on complex engineering diagrams. We define two tasks over a corpus of U.S. military service and repair manuals: structured parts-table extraction (Task 1) and free-form visual diagram question answering (VQA)(Task 2) for benchmarking. We evaluate four frontier VLMs (GPT-5.2 Chat, Claude Opus 4.7, Gemma 4, Qwen3-VL-32B-Instruct) under zero-shot and chain-of-thought prompting. On Task 1, models reach Recall@all of 0.61-0.87 but Token F1pen of only 0.03-0.18, exposing a systematic gap between part identification and description fidelity. Task 2 reveals a consistent factual-reasoning gap across all models. A supporting analysis shows that token-overlap metrics under-report model capability on technical descriptions by 2-6x relative to semantic similarity, motivating LLM-as-judge calibration for domain-specific evaluation. We release the dataset, annotations, evaluation harness, and per-sample model outputs to support a reproducible study of VLM capability on engineering content.

</details>


### [56] [A unified multi-task framework enables interpretable chest radiograph analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03417)
*Lijian Xu,Ziyu Ni,Xinglong Liu,Xiaosong Wang,Hongsheng Li,Shaoting Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new framework, IMT-CXR, is proposed for interpretable multi-task chest X-ray analysis, emulating radiologists' diagnostic workflow and providing traceable decision pathways.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing black-box systems for medical imaging analysis may overlook the trust-sensitive nature of clinical diagnosis as a multi-task process.

Method: The IMT-CXR framework employs a unified transformer architecture optimized via medical-domain instruction tuning, sequentially executing four clinical tasks.

Result: The framework demonstrates competitive performance on ten CXR benchmarks and achieves 66% of AI-generated reports rated as comparable to or surpassing original clinical reports in diagnostic clarity.

Conclusion: The proposed framework bridges the gap between AI technical metrics and clinical utility, advancing trustworthy AI systems in medical imaging.

Abstract: While multimodal deep learning has advanced medical imaging analysis, existing black-box systems \textcolor{black}{may remain confined to isolated tasks, often overlooking} the trust-sensitive nature of clinical diagnosis as a multi-task process. We propose IMT-CXR (Interpretable Multi-task Transformer for Chest X-ray Analysis), a framework that emulates radiologists' diagnostic workflow through three evidence-driven stages: 1) Disease recognition; 2) Attribute characterization (e.g., size, location, severity quantification); 3) Evidence-integrated report generation with traceable decision pathways. The framework employs a unified transformer architecture optimized via medical-domain instruction tuning, sequentially executing four clinical tasks: multi-label disease classification, lesion localization, anatomical segmentation, and radiology report generation. Experimental validation demonstrates competitive performance on ten CXR benchmarks under direct inference and fine-tuning settings. In a blinded evaluation of 160 historical reports from four medical centers, three radiologists rated 66\% of AI-generated reports as comparable to or surpassing original clinical reports in diagnostic clarity, highlighting the framework's translational potential. By establishing traceable diagnostic pathways from anatomical findings to conclusions, this work bridges the gap between AI technical metrics and clinical utility, advancing trustworthy AI systems in medical imaging.

</details>


### [57] [IDO: Incongruity-aware Distribution Optimization for Multimodal Fake News Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03418)
*Hengyang Zhou,Rongman Hong,Yuxuan Zhou,Jing Wang,Zhaoyan Pan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposed Incongruity-aware Distribution Optimization (IDO) for multimodal fake news detection, addressing factual and modality incongruity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods fail to explicitly model semantic incongruity in deceptive multimodal content, which often contains semantic information incongruity with facts.

Method: IDO uses channel-wise reweighting for semantically discriminative embeddings, gaussian distribution for uncertain correlation, and incongruity contrastive learning for cross-modal semantic information.

Result: IDO achieves state-of-the-art performance in multimodal fake news detection.

Conclusion: IDO effectively improves fake news detection by addressing factual and modality incongruity.

Abstract: Multimodal fake news detection aims to identify the authenticity of news. Existing multimodal fake news detection methods mainly focus on cross-modal consistency, but often fail to explicitly model the semantic incongruity that characterizes deceptive multimodal content. However, misinformation often contains semantic information incongruity with the facts. To address these challenges, we propose Incongruity-aware Distribution Optimization (IDO) to improve the performance of fake news detection from the perspectives of factual incongruity and modality incongruity. For factual incongruity, we introduce a channel-wise reweighting strategy to obtain semantically discriminative embeddings and utilize gaussian distribution to model the uncertain correlation caused by factual incongruity. For modality incongruity, we utilize incongruity contrastive learning to learn cross-modal semantic information. Experiments demonstrate that IDO achieves state-of-the-art performance.

</details>


### [58] [PHAF-Personalized Hand Avatars in a Flash](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03420)
*Meghana Shankar,Akanxit Upadhyay,Anmol Namdev,Green Rosh KS,Pawan Prasad BH*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new method for generating personalized, photo-realistic hand avatars in real-time, using just two images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current methods for generating personalized hand avatars are slow and not suitable for real-time deployment on edge devices.

Method: PHAF combines semantic guided mesh alignment, densified texture extraction, and a view-based inpainting network to generate personalized textures.

Result: PHAF achieves comparable visual fidelity to existing methods, but with a 30x reduction in texture generation time.

Conclusion: PHAF enables practical AR/VR applications with fast and high-quality personalized hand avatars.
